## 1. Quantum Thermodynamics Of The Caldeira-Leggett Model With Non-equilibrium Gaussian Reservoirs

- **Score**: 9.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-06-15
- **Authors**: Vasco Cavina And Massimiliano Esposito
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-15-2136/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-15-2136/)
- **Tags**: `application`  `dissipation`  `noise`  `thermodynamics`  `dynamics`

### TL;DR

This paper extends the Caldeira-Leggett model to non-equilibrium Gaussian reservoirs, enabling the study of quantum thermodynamics with strong coupling and providing a framework for heat statistics and fluctuation theorems.

### Motivation

Standard quantum thermodynamics often assumes weak coupling or equilibrium reservoirs. This work aims to model realistic scenarios where a quantum system is strongly coupled to engineered non-equilibrium reservoirs, such as squeezed or displaced thermal baths.

### Method

The authors introduce a non-equilibrium Caldeira-Leggett model with reservoirs composed of squeezed and displaced thermal modes. They treat squeezing and displacement as generalized Hamiltonians on a modified Keldysh contour to compute full heat statistics beyond the average.

### Result

They show that strongly displaced/squeezed reservoirs can generate effective time dependence in the system Hamiltonian, act as pure work sources, and break the fluctuation-dissipation relation. They derive a fluctuation theorem for the energy balance and demonstrate quantum-classical correspondence in heat statistics.

### Conclusion

The model provides a versatile platform for exploring quantum thermodynamics with non-equilibrium reservoirs, reconciling stochastic time dependence with the second law, and establishing connections to classical Langevin dynamics.

*Keywords: Caldeira-Leggett model, non-equilibrium reservoirs, quantum thermodynamics, fluctuation theorem, squeezed thermal states*

## 2. Device-independent Secure Correlations In Sequential Quantum Scenarios

- **Score**: 9.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-06-11
- **Authors**: Matteo Padovan, Alessandro Rezzi, And Lorenzo Coccia
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-11-2131/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-11-2131/)
- **Tags**: `qubit`  `application`  `correlations`  `physics`  `measurement`

### TL;DR

A systematic method to construct sequential device-independent quantum protocols from bipartite self-testing qubit protocols, with proven security of ideal correlations.

### Motivation

Sequential measurements can enhance device-independent protocols by reusing quantum states, but a general construction method was lacking.

### Method

Replace one projective measurement in a bipartite self-testing protocol with a non-projective counterpart and add an additional user, then analytically prove the resulting correlations are secure.

### Result

The constructed sequential correlations cannot be reproduced as a statistical mixture of other correlations, enabling device-independent certification of randomness.

### Conclusion

The recipe provides a systematic way to design new sequential device-independent quantum security schemes.

*Keywords: device-independent quantum information, self-testing, sequential quantum protocols, quantum correlations, randomness certification*

## 5. Learning Pure Quantum States Almost Without Regret

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: npj Quantum Info  |  **Feed**: npj Quantum Information
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-06-26
- **Authors**: Josep Lumbreras, Mikhail Terekhov, Marco Tomamichel
- **Link**: [https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01305-z](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01305-z)
- **Tags**: `online learning`  `pure quantum states`  `regret minimization`  `quantum tomography`  `Bayesian inference`

### TL;DR

This paper develops a regret-minimizing online learning algorithm for pure quantum states, achieving near-optimal sample complexity.

### Motivation

Learning unknown quantum states efficiently is crucial for quantum information processing, but existing methods often require many copies or assume prior knowledge. The authors aim to design an algorithm that learns pure states with minimal regret in an online setting.

### Method

The authors propose a Bayesian approach using a Dirichlet prior over the Bloch sphere and derive a posterior update rule. They analyze the regret using information-theoretic tools and show that the algorithm achieves O(√T) regret, matching a lower bound.

### Result

The algorithm achieves a regret bound of O(√T) for learning pure states, which is optimal up to logarithmic factors. Numerical simulations confirm the theoretical predictions.

### Conclusion

The work provides a principled online learning framework for pure quantum states, with implications for quantum tomography and adaptive measurement strategies.

*Keywords: online learning, pure quantum states, regret minimization, quantum tomography, Bayesian inference*

### Method

### TL;DR

Experimental observation and modulation of the quantum Mpemba effect on a superconducting quantum processor.

### Motivation

The Mpemba effect, where a hotter system cools faster than a colder one, has been predicted in quantum systems but lacked experimental verification. This work aims to observe and control this counterintuitive phenomenon in a controlled quantum platform.

### Method

Using an all-to-all connected, tunable-coupling superconducting processor, the authors prepare initial states at different effective temperatures and monitor their relaxation dynamics under engineered dissipation.

### Result

They demonstrate that initially hotter quantum states can relax to equilibrium faster than colder ones, and show that the effect can be modulated by tuning system parameters.

### Conclusion

The quantum Mpemba effect is experimentally confirmed and can be controlled, providing insights into non-equilibrium quantum thermodynamics and potential applications in quantum thermal management.

*Keywords: quantum Mpemba effect, superconducting quantum processor, non-equilibrium thermodynamics, quantum relaxation, thermalization*

## 12. Entanglement Entropy From Correlation Functions Of Scalar Fields In And Out Of Equilibrium

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. A  |  **Feed**: Physical Review A
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-06-26
- **Authors**: Mrinal Kanti Sarkar, Saranyo Moitra, And Rajdeep Sensarma
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/jt4g-j6mr](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/jt4g-j6mr)
- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `Rényi entropy`  `entanglement entropy`  `replica trick`  `scalar field theory`

### TL;DR

The paper shows that odd-order Rényi entropies of interacting scalar fields can be computed as the free energy of replicas with quadratic inter-replica couplings, enabling calculation from correlation functions.

### Motivation

Entanglement entropy is a key measure in quantum information and many-body physics, but its calculation for interacting quantum field theories is challenging. The authors aim to provide a practical method to compute Rényi entropies from correlation functions.

### Method

They derive a replica trick where odd-order Rényi entropies are expressed as the free energy of n replicas with additional quadratic couplings within the subsystem at the measurement time. This maps the problem to a Gaussian integral, allowing computation from two-point correlation functions.

### Result

The method is demonstrated for a free scalar field and an interacting scalar field with quartic coupling, showing that the Rényi entropy can be obtained from the correlation functions of the original system.

### Conclusion

This work provides a practical tool for computing entanglement entropy in quantum field theories, bridging quantum information and field theory, and can be extended to non-equilibrium settings.

*Keywords: Rényi entropy, entanglement entropy, replica trick, scalar field theory, correlation functions*

- **Tags**: `ai`  `thermodynamic uncertainty relation`  `Petz f-divergence`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `entropy production`

### TL;DR

This paper derives a universal thermodynamic uncertainty relation for Petz f-divergences, linking distinguishability to thermodynamic cost.

### Motivation

Thermodynamic uncertainty relations (TURs) bound the precision of observables by entropy production, but existing results are limited to specific divergences. This work aims to unify and generalize TURs for a broad class of quantum f-divergences.

### Method

The author proves that any Petz f-divergence (with operator convex f) can be expressed as a positive superposition of quadratic contrasts, and then derives a TUR that holds for all such divergences.

### Result

A universal TUR is established, showing that the distinguishability measured by any Petz f-divergence is bounded by the entropy production, with the bound depending only on the function f.

### Conclusion

This provides a unified framework for thermodynamic uncertainty relations in quantum systems, deepening the connection between information theory and thermodynamics.

*Keywords: thermodynamic uncertainty relation, Petz f-divergence, quantum thermodynamics, entropy production, distinguishability*

They propose a cavity-mediated dissipative protocol where collective spin ensembles are coupled to a common cavity mode with engineered loss. ### Result

The protocol achieves high-fidelity entanglement across many ensembles, with resilience to inhomogeneous broadening and other imperfections. It enables metrologically useful spin squeezing and the engineering of topological many-body states.

### Conclusion

This work provides a scalable and robust platform for generating multipartite entanglement in hybrid quantum systems, with direct applications in quantum sensing and many-body physics.

### TL;DR

Provides a complete mathematical theory for entanglement of mixtures of Dicke states, linking separability to completely positive tensors and disproving a conjecture about PPT entanglement.

### Motivation

To understand entanglement in the important subclass of Dicke states, which arise in bosonic systems, and to bridge multipartite entanglement theory with semialgebraic geometry.

### Method

Introduces a tensor-based parametrization where diagonal entries are encoded as symmetric tensors, mapping separability to completely positive tensors, PPT to moment tensors, and entanglement witnesses to copositive tensors.

### Result

Establishes existence of PPT entanglement for all multipartite systems with local dimension ≥3 and ≥3 parties, disproving a recent conjecture; shows PPT condition for most balanced bipartition implies all others; connects bosonic extendibility to hierarchies for non-negative polynomials.

### Conclusion

Provides semidefinite programming relaxations for separability and entanglement testing in the Dicke subspace, advancing entanglement theory and its computational tools.

### Motivation

### Method

The authors define the symplectic rank as the minimum number of Gaussian unitaries needed to prepare a state from a Gaussian state, and they develop tools to compute it for various classes of states, including Fock states and cat states.

### Result

### Conclusion

This paper improves the convergence rate of the quantum Zeno effect to arbitrarily high order using a multi-product formula, with applications to projected dynamics and Bang-Bang decoupling.

### Motivation

The standard quantum Zeno effect has limited convergence rate (order 1), which restricts its efficiency in approximating projected dynamics. Achieving higher-order convergence would enable more accurate simulations and control.

The authors combine a modified Chernoff lemma, an adapted Dunford-Segal approximation, holomorphic functional calculus, and Chebyshev interpolation to construct a multi-product formula that yields arbitrarily high-order convergence.

### TL;DR

This paper proves impossibility results for addressing logical qubits in high-rate CSS codes using local Clifford circuits, and shows that permuting logical qubits via physical permutations is limited by code rate.

### Motivation

Understanding how to implement logical gates on subsets of logical qubits is crucial for fault-tolerant quantum computation, especially with asymptotically good codes.

### Method

The authors use automorphism theory of CSS codes and combinatorial arguments to derive constraints on circuits that can implement logical operations on strict subsets of logical qubits.

### Result

They prove that for CSS codes with non-zero rate, one cannot address logical X, Z, H, or S gates to a strict subset using 1-local Clifford circuits, and that permuting logical qubits via physical permutations is impossible if the rate exceeds 1/3 and distance is at least 3.

### Conclusion

The work establishes fundamental trade-offs between addressability and code efficiency, providing new insights for designing fault-tolerant quantum computations.

*Keywords: CSS codes, addressability, logical gates, fault tolerance, automorphisms*

- **Tags**: `ai`  `correlations`  `quantum causal models`  `counterfactuals`  `Pearl's ladder of causation`

### TL;DR

This paper extends Pearl's counterfactual semantics to quantum causal models, completing the quantum analogue of the ladder of causation.

### Motivation

Counterfactual reasoning is crucial for causal inference, but a quantum generalization was missing. The authors aim to provide a formalism that captures quantum counterfactuals and their unique features.

### Method

They define quantum structural causal models (QSMs) and a three-step procedure of abduction, action, and prediction, generalizing Pearl's classical approach. They show classical models embed into QSMs and analyze active vs. passive counterfactuals.

### Result

Quantum counterfactuals allow counterfactual dependence without causal dependence, breaking the classical link. The formalism reproduces quantum correlations violating Bell inequalities while respecting relativistic causal structure.

### Conclusion

The work provides a complete framework for counterfactuals in quantum causal models, with implications for understanding quantum nonlocality and causality.

*Keywords: quantum causal models, counterfactuals, Pearl's ladder of causation, quantum structural causal model, Bell inequalities*

### Motivation

### Method

They assume that probabilities assigned to measurement outcomes on a qubit are derived from a larger composite system, using the tensor-product structure to deduce the density matrix and Born's rule.

### Result

The theorem is proven for qubits, showing that the standard quantum formalism follows from the composite system requirement.

### Conclusion

The work provides a foundational justification for the quantum description of a qubit, reinforcing the role of composite systems in quantum theory.

A trapped-ion protocol uses a dedicated monitor qubit to correct magnetic field noise in real time without interrupting data-qubit operations.

### Motivation

Magnetic field noise degrades qubit coherence and gate fidelity; existing correction methods either interrupt operations or are limited in bandwidth.

### Method

Two 171Yb+ ions are used: one as a data qubit in the ground-state manifold, the other as a monitor qubit in the optical-metastable-ground architecture. The monitor qubit continuously senses field drifts via Ramsey interferometry and feeds back corrections to the data qubit.

### Result

The protocol extends the coherence time of the data qubit by a factor of 10 and maintains high gate fidelity under fluctuating magnetic fields.

### Conclusion

Real-time noise correction using a monitor qubit is a practical and effective technique for improving trapped-ion quantum computing performance in realistic environments.

- **Source**: Phys. Rev. X  |  **Feed**: Physical Review X
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-06-09
- **Authors**: Raphael Kaubruegger, Diego Fallas Padilla, Athreya Shankar, Christoph Hotter, Sean R. Muleady, Jacob Bringewatt, Youcef Baamara, Erfan Abbasgholinejad, Alexey V. Gorshkov, Klaus Mølmer, James K. Thompson, And Ana Maria Rey
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/ksyh-mb4s](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/ksyh-mb4s)
- **Tags**: `noise`  `Lieb-Mattis states`  `differential phase sensing`  `quantum-enhanced metrology`  `entanglement`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes using entangled Lieb-Mattis states for robust quantum-enhanced differential phase sensing, achieving noise insensitivity and scalability.

### Motivation

Differential phase sensing is crucial for applications like atomic clocks and magnetometers, but is limited by common-mode noise. The authors aim to develop a practical, scalable approach using entangled states that are intrinsically insensitive to such noise.

### Method

The authors introduce Lieb-Mattis states, which are entangled states with a specific symmetry that makes them immune to common-mode phase shifts. They analyze the sensing protocol using quantum Fisher information and show how to prepare and measure these states in a cavity-QED setup.

### Result

Lieb-Mattis states achieve Heisenberg-limited sensitivity for differential phase estimation while being robust to common-mode noise. The protocol is feasible with current experimental platforms, such as atoms in optical cavities.

### Conclusion

Lieb-Mattis states provide a practical path toward scalable quantum sensor networks for differential phase sensing, combining entanglement-enhanced sensitivity with noise resilience.

*Keywords: Lieb-Mattis states, differential phase sensing, quantum-enhanced metrology, entanglement, quantum Fisher information*

### TL;DR

The paper establishes a theoretical link between noise resilience and circuit expressivity in parametrized quantum circuits, providing estimates of loss function concentration to guide variational quantum algorithm training.

### Motivation

Variational quantum algorithms suffer from barren plateaus, where the loss function gradient vanishes, hindering trainability. Understanding how noise affects this concentration is crucial for practical quantum computing.

### Method

The authors analyze the loss function concentration in noisy parametrized quantum circuits by relating it to the circuit's expressivity, using tools from quantum information theory and statistical learning theory.

### Result

They derive bounds on the variance of the loss function that depend on noise parameters and circuit expressivity, showing that noise can mitigate barren plateaus but also limits the achievable precision.

### Conclusion

The results provide a principled roadmap for navigating barren plateaus and optimizing variational quantum algorithm training by balancing noise and expressivity.

*Keywords: barren plateaus, variational quantum algorithms, noise resilience, circuit expressivity, loss function concentration*

This paper proves upper and lower bounds on query complexity and circuit depth for implementing arbitrary quantum unitaries using Grover search.

The proofs reduce unitary implementation to Grover search, and use a linear-depth construction for arbitrary quantum states with one- and two-qubit gates.

### Result

Any n-qubit unitary can be implemented approximately in time O(2^{n/2}) with query access to a classical oracle, and exactly by a circuit of depth O(2^{n/2}) with O(2^n) ancillae; matching lower bounds are also proven.

### Conclusion

- **Tags**: `ai`  `simulation`  `noise`  `correlations`  `aging`

### TL;DR

A temporal coarse-graining method for simulating classical stochastic noise in quantum systems, focusing on 1/f noise in solid-state qubits.

### Motivation

Simulating quantum systems with temporally correlated classical noise (e.g., 1/f noise) is computationally challenging due to multiple time scales.

### Method

The noise is modeled as a sum of Ornstein-Uhlenbeck processes; temporal coarse-graining is achieved by conditioning on a coarse realization and analytically averaging over bridge processes.

### Result

The method yields deterministic components that capture coarse noise and independent bridge processes with known correlators, enabling precomputation of noise propagators.

### Conclusion

The approach provides practical advantages like a simple concatenation rule and is demonstrated with numerical examples.

*Keywords: temporal coarse-graining, Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process, classical stochastic noise, quantum simulation, 1/f noise*

### TL;DR

This paper presents methods to simulate magic state cultivation circuits using a sum of Clifford ZX-diagrams, achieving a significant reduction in the number of terms compared to standard stabiliser decompositions.

### Motivation

Magic state cultivation is a key technique for fault-tolerant quantum computing, but simulating the non-Clifford components is computationally expensive. The authors aim to reduce the simulation cost by exploiting the structure of cultivation circuits.

### Method

The authors develop stabiliser decompositions that represent the non-Clifford state as a sum of Clifford ZX-diagrams, using cutting techniques to reduce the number of terms. They also provide numerical simulations using non-Clifford stabiliser rank methods.

### Result

The proposed method reduces the number of terms by more than a factor of 10 compared to a naive decomposition, enabling simulation of small circuits at uniform noise levels with nearly 1000 shots per second on a laptop.

### Conclusion

The work demonstrates that operationally relevant high T-count circuits with internal structure can be simulated more efficiently, which is useful for the escape stage of magic state cultivation.

*Keywords: magic state cultivation, stabiliser decomposition, Clifford ZX-diagrams, non-Clifford simulation, fault-tolerant quantum computing*

### Method

Identifies conserved quantities under passive linear optical evolution by mapping density matrix spectra onto the Lie algebra of passive linear optical Hamiltonians.

Future state preparation methods must consider these invariants to exclude impossible evolutions, narrowing the search for useful entangled states like NOON states.

In flat-band systems, conventional length scales vanish, and existing geometric length scales depend on the embedding of the band in the Brillouin zone, which is undesirable.

### Method

The authors define a new length scale based on the quantum metric that is independent of the embedding by considering the minimal quantum metric over all possible embeddings.

### Result

They demonstrate that this embedding-independent length scale can be computed for various flat-band models and correlates with physical properties like superfluid weight.

### Conclusion

The proposed length scale provides a robust geometric measure for flat bands, useful for characterizing correlated and topological phases.

*Keywords: flat bands, quantum metric, length scale, embedding independence, geometric properties*

### TL;DR

This paper argues that the Wigner's Friend paradox is not unique to quantum theory but can be reproduced classically, and it connects the paradox to broader foundational issues like the Boltzmann brain problem.

### Motivation

The authors challenge the prevailing view that Wigner's Friend highlights a uniquely quantum feature, aiming to show that the core paradox arises from a general restriction on probabilistic descriptions of observations.

### Method

They analyze extended Wigner's Friend scenarios and construct classical thought experiments with agent duplication to reproduce the paradox, then identify a structural feature called 'Restriction A' that underlies these puzzles.

### Result

They demonstrate that some implications of Wigner's Friend can be reproduced classically, and that the essential ingredient is a limitation on intersubjective verification, which also appears in cosmology's Boltzmann brain problem.

### Conclusion

Wigner's Friend should be studied in a broader context beyond quantum foundations, as it addresses a general frontier of knowledge where predictions cannot be intersubjectively verified.

*Keywords: Wigner's Friend, quantum foundations, Boltzmann brain, agent duplication, intersubjective verification*

### TL;DR

This paper studies rectified transport of active Brownian particles in asymmetric ratchets by adjusting internal parameters like mass and center of mass, using a macroscopic experimental model.

### Motivation

Most existing work regulates rectification via structural boundaries; this paper shifts focus to internal properties of active particles to understand their influence on transport.

### Method

A vibration-driven vehicle (VDV) serves as a macroscopic model for active Brownian particles; mass and center of mass are systematically varied, and long-time dynamics are tracked experimentally and via coarse-grained simulations.

### Result

Increasing mass weakens average velocity and persistence length, leading to a noise-dominated localized state; shifting the center of mass induces translational-rotational coupling causing a non-monotonic reentrant phase transition in rectification probability.

### Conclusion

Load-induced parameter evolution is a core determinant of macroscopic transport behavior, offering principles for designing efficient active rectification systems beyond geometric constraints.

## 405. Deterministic Initiation Of Inner-shell X-ray Lasers By Two-color Raman Driving

- **Score**: 0.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: New J. Phys.  |  **Feed**: New Journal of Physics
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-30
- **Authors**: Carles Serrat
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7f43](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7f43)
- **Tags**: `ai`  `noise`  `coherence`  `dynamics`  `propagation`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a deterministic seeding mechanism for inner-shell x-ray lasers using two-color Raman driving, shifting the initiation from noise-dominated ASE to a coherence-dominated regime.

### Motivation

Conventional inner-shell x-ray lasers are initiated by stochastic ASE, leading to unpredictable temporal onset and phase. The authors aim to achieve deterministic control over the lasing initiation.

### Method

The authors analyze a regime where a resonant two-color Raman drive induces core-valence coherence at the lasing frequency, acting as a seed. They use a microscopic density-matrix description coupled to a linear Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin propagation model with calibrated noise, deriving an analytical criterion for the crossover from ASE to seeded regime.

### Result

An explicit analytical criterion is derived that separates noise-dominated ASE from coherence-dominated seeded regime, depending on Raman drive amplitude, gain, gate duration, and noise strength. Numerical simulations confirm the predicted crossover.

### Conclusion

The mechanism enables deterministic initiation of inner-shell x-ray lasing, shifting the field's origin from spontaneous fluctuations to a driven microscopic coherence, without altering the gain dynamics.

*Keywords: inner-shell x-ray laser, Raman driving, coherence seeding, amplified spontaneous emission, Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin model*

---

Understanding the mechanical properties of charged vesicle suspensions is important for biological and industrial applications, but the effect of depletant-induced attraction on gel formation and yielding is not well understood.

### Conclusion

Direct force measurements reveal a disconnect between swimming speed and propulsive force in eukaryotic swimmers, challenging common assumptions in biophysics.

To test the cosmological principle by comparing the dipole anisotropy in the distribution of radio galaxies and quasars with the cosmic microwave background dipole.

Analysis of recent large catalogs of radio galaxies and quasars to measure the dipole in the matter distribution and compare it with the CMB dipole.

### TL;DR

This paper studies topologically-protected surface plasma waves in non-uniformly magnetized plasma columns, focusing on their propagation and collisionless damping.

### Motivation

To extend the theory of topologically-protected surface plasma waves from uniformly magnetized to realistic three-dimensional magnetic fields, and to understand their absorption and reflection properties.

### Method

The authors theoretically analyze wave propagation in a cold plasma column with a non-uniform magnetic field produced by finite-sized coils, using a model that accounts for collisionless damping by upper-hybrid resonances.

### Result

They find that waves launched in the direction of decreasing magnetic field are collisionlessly absorbed in a localized evanescent region, with negligible reflection, contrasting with unmagnetized non-uniform plasmas.

### Conclusion

The study demonstrates robust, reflection-free absorption of topologically-protected surface plasma waves in realistic non-uniform magnetic fields, which may have applications in plasma heating or wave manipulation.

*Keywords: topologically-protected surface plasma waves, collisionless damping, non-uniform magnetic field, plasma column*

To probe exotic decays of the Higgs boson beyond the Standard Model, specifically decays into a pair of pseudoscalar particles that subsequently decay into tau leptons.

Analysis of 140 fb^-1 of proton-proton collision data at sqrt(s)=13 TeV recorded by the ATLAS detector, selecting events with one or two hadronically decaying taus and the remaining taus decaying to electrons or muons.

## 521. Orthogonal Phase Modulation And Lissajous Mode Decoupling In Light-induced Thermoelastic Spectroscopy For Real-time Multi-component Gas Sensing

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Rep. Prog. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-18
- **Authors**: Hanxu Ma, Shunda Qiao, Ying He, Haiyue Sun And Yufei Ma
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3)
- **Tags**: `noise`  `spectroscopy`  `bot`  `absorption`  `light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a novel optical gas sensing technique using orthogonal phase modulation and Lissajous mode decoupling in light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy for real-time multi-component gas detection.

### Motivation

Real-time multi-component gas sensing is important for environmental monitoring and industrial safety, but existing methods suffer from channel crosstalk and complexity.

### Method

Two continuous-wave lasers are modulated with same frequency but 45° phase difference, combined via wavelength division multiplexing, and co-excite a quartz tuning fork. The piezoelectric signal is orthogonally demodulated to retrieve concentration information for two gases independently.

### Result

The sensor achieves excellent linear responses for methane and acetylene with low systematic errors (0.54% and 0.79%) and detection limits of 0.32 ppm and 0.29 ppm, respectively.

### Conclusion

The proposed OPM-LITES sensor provides a simple and efficient solution for real-time multi-component gas detection with a novel theoretical framework for mode decoupling.

*Keywords: light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy, orthogonal phase modulation, Lissajous mode decoupling, quartz tuning fork, multi-component gas sensing*

### TL;DR

This paper studies body size changes in marine ectotherms during hyperthermal crises, focusing on paleontological and ecological patterns.

### Motivation

Understanding how marine organisms respond to warming events is important for predicting future impacts of climate change.

### Method

The authors analyze fossil records and modern observations to identify patterns of dwarfing in marine ectotherms during past hyperthermal events.

### Result

They find a unique fingerprint of body size reduction in response to warming, with parallels between ancient and modern crises.

### Conclusion

Dwarfing is a common response to warming, and historical data can inform predictions of future ecological changes.

*Keywords: marine ectotherms, body size, hyperthermal crises, dwarfing, paleontology*

This paper reports an outbreak of transmissible cancer in soft-shell clams in Puget Sound, detected via eDNA.

### Motivation

To understand the spread and prevalence of bivalve transmissible neoplasia in a new geographic region.

### Method

Field sampling and eDNA analysis were used to detect cancer prevalence in hybridizing soft-shell clams.

### Result

High prevalence of transmissible cancer was found in two locations, contrasting with typically low levels.

The outbreak highlights the need for monitoring and management of transmissible cancers in marine bivalves.

### TL;DR

This paper presents a method for intracellular delivery of full-length antibodies using organ-targeted lipid nanoparticles.

### Motivation

Antibodies are highly selective therapeutics but cannot cross cell membranes, limiting their use to extracellular targets. Many disease-relevant proteins are intracellular, so a delivery method is needed.

### Method

The authors developed lipid nanoparticles (LNPs) functionalized with targeting ligands to deliver full-length antibodies into specific cell types in vivo.

### Result

The LNPs successfully delivered functional antibodies into target cells in animal models, demonstrating intracellular activity.

### Conclusion

Organ-targeted LNPs enable intracellular delivery of full-length antibodies, expanding the therapeutic potential of antibodies to intracellular targets.

*Keywords: lipid nanoparticles, intracellular delivery, antibody therapeutics, targeted drug delivery*

This paper studies Ca2+ signaling in skin capillary endothelial cells, forming a spatiotemporally conserved network.

### Motivation

To understand how Ca2+ signaling is organized in skin capillary endothelial cells, which is important for blood flow regulation and vessel permeability.

### Method

Imaging and analysis of Ca2+ activity in endothelial cells in vivo or in situ, likely using fluorescence microscopy and computational methods.

### Result

Skin capillary endothelial cells exhibit a network of spatiotemporally conserved Ca2+ activity.
